What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. I work as a flagger, and lately... I will just look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I cannot remember the steps. It is just gone. These are things I have done for years, and I just completely blank on what to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these memory blanks only when you're working as a flagger, or do they happen in other situations as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it is mostly at work where I really notice it, because it happens at the absolute worst times. Like I was just saying, I will be staring right at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my brain just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the basic steps for things I have done for years. I guess I might lose my train of thought at home too, but on the job is where the blanking is really causing a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the blanking happens because you're feeling stressed or anxious in those situations at work?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I've been doing this job for a long time, so I'm not really nervous or anxious out there. It just... it happens out of nowhere, even on a totally normal, quiet day. So I wouldn't say it's from being stressed. I just don't know why it's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue when these memory blanks happen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I suddenly cannot remember the basic steps for things I have done for years. I used to be pretty involved with things. I was always the one organizing rides for the choir, setting up garden swaps, or getting snacks together for the youth basketball games. But now... From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. I work as a flagger, and lately... I will just look at the road-closure forms or my stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I cannot remember the steps. It is just gone. These are things I have done for years, and I just completely blank on what to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these memory blanks only when you're working as a flagger, or do they happen in other situations as well?\n\nPatient: Well, it is mostly at work where I really notice it, because it happens at the absolute worst times. Like I was just saying, I will be staring right at the road-closure forms or holding my stop-slow paddle, and my brain just completely empties out. I suddenly cannot remember the basic steps for things I have done for years. I guess I might lose my train of thought at home too, but on the job is where the blanking is really causing a problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the blanking happens because you're feeling stressed or anxious in those situations at work?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I've been doing this job for a long time, so I'm not really nervous or anxious out there. It just... it happens out of nowhere, even on a totally normal, quiet day. So I wouldn't say it's from being stressed. I just don't know why it's happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any physical symptoms like headaches or fatigue when these memory blanks happen?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My head doesn't hurt, and I don't feel suddenly exhausted or anything right when it happens. Physically, I feel fine... it is really just my mind that completely stops working.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Actually, most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow sadness right in my chest. It just feels like... like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been waking up with this heavy, hollow sadness?\n\nPatient: It's been about a year and two months now. And it's really not just that heavy depression, either... for that whole time, I've also been dealing with this constant guilt, really low energy, and I've pretty much lost my appetite. But yeah, all of that has been with me for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anxious. I mean, I don't sit around worrying about everyday things or my job or anything like that. It's really just that heavy sadness I mentioned taking over everything. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worried or panicked.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to be pretty involved with things. I was always the one organizing rides for the choir, setting up garden swaps, or getting snacks together for the youth basketball games. But now... I just don't have the drive for any of it. I can sit there for hours just staring at my phone, and I don't even feel the push to answer anybody when they reach out. I just don't care about doing those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I just don't have the energy to fight the thought anymore that it might be better if I just didn't wake up. I've actually started saying it out loud, which... I know that scares my family. But it's just how I feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of worsening of these depressive symptoms over the past fourteen months?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I can pinpoint specific times where it got worse. I haven't really noticed it going up and down, just that it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, both, actually. Most of the time, I'm just... really slowed down. It takes me so long just to bathe and get dressed and make it to the door. My family says it looks like I'm moving underwater. But then, other times, I get this... I don't know, this restless feeling. I end up just pacing up and down the hallway, wringing my hands, and I just can't seem to settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where these depressive symptoms were so severe that you were unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's hard, and like I said, it takes me forever to get going, but I haven't had days where I physically couldn't get out of bed at all. I still manage to get up, even if it's just to sit on the couch or pace the hallway.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like you were at the lowest point, with your depressive symptoms being the worst they've ever been?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying before, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a specific low point where it suddenly got worse than usual. It's just... always severe, always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve or get better, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't ever really lifted. Looking back, most of this whole illness has just been spent under that heavy depression. Even during the times when the voices or the suspicious thoughts were quieter, that dark, heavy feeling was still there. It just hasn't gotten any better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your depressive symptoms were somewhat better for at least two months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, it's just been constant. I haven't had a break from it for two months, or even two weeks. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since your depressive symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've lost weight. Food has just... stopped meaning much to me lately. I guess I skip meals without even really noticing it. The only reason I realized is because my flagger pants for work are getting pretty loose on me, and my family started asking why I look so drawn out. So, yes, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness and depression present for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they are. It's pretty much all day, every day. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy... just this hollow sadness right in my chest. It feels like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor. Actually, most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow sadness right in my chest. It just feels like... like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been waking up with this heavy, hollow sadness?\n\nPatient: It's been about a year and two months now. And it's really not just that heavy depression, either... for that whole time, I've also been dealing with this constant guilt, really low energy, and I've pretty much lost my appetite. But yeah, all of that has been with me for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or worried about things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get anxious. I mean, I don't sit around worrying about everyday things or my job or anything like that. It's really just that heavy sadness I mentioned taking over everything. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worried or panicked.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to be pretty involved with things. I was always the one organizing rides for the choir, setting up garden swaps, or getting snacks together for the youth basketball games. But now... I just don't have the drive for any of it. I can sit there for hours just staring at my phone, and I don't even feel the push to answer anybody when they reach out. I just don't care about doing those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I just don't have the energy to fight the thought anymore that it might be better if I just didn't wake up. I've actually started saying it out loud, which... I know that scares my family. But it's just how I feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of worsening of these depressive symptoms over the past fourteen months?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I can pinpoint specific times where it got worse. It feels like it's just been this... constant, heavy thing the whole time. I haven't really noticed it going up and down, just that it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, both, actually. Most of the time, I'm just... really slowed down. It takes me so long just to bathe and get dressed and make it to the door. My family says it looks like I'm moving underwater. But then, other times, I get this... I don't know, this restless feeling. I end up just pacing up and down the hallway, wringing my hands, and I just can't seem to settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where these depressive symptoms were so severe that you were unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's hard, and like I said, it takes me forever to get going, but I haven't had days where I physically couldn't get out of bed at all. I still manage to get up, even if it's just to sit on the couch or pace the hallway.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like you were at the lowest point, with your depressive symptoms being the worst they've ever been?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying before, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a specific low point where it suddenly got worse than usual. It's just... always severe, always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve or get better, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't ever really lifted. Looking back, most of this whole illness has just been spent under that heavy depression. Even during the times when the voices or the suspicious thoughts were quieter, that dark, heavy feeling was still there. It just hasn't gotten any better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your depressive symptoms were somewhat better for at least two months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, it's just been constant. I haven't had a break from it for two months, or even two weeks. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since your depressive symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've lost weight. Food has just... stopped meaning much to me lately. I guess I skip meals without even really noticing it. The only reason I realized is because my flagger pants for work are getting pretty loose on me, and my family started asking why I look so drawn out. So, yes, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness and depression present for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they are. It's pretty much all day, every day. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy... just this hollow sadness right in my chest. It feels like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor. Everything is just so slow. It takes me so long just to bathe, get dressed, and make it to the door. I used to be pretty involved with things. I was always the one organizing rides for the choir, setting up garden swaps, or getting snacks together for the youth basketball games. But now... I just don't have the drive for any of it. I can sit there for hours just staring at my phone, and I don't even feel the push to answer anybody when they reach out. I just don't care about doing those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I just don't have the energy to fight the thought anymore that it might be better if I just didn't wake up. I've actually started saying it out loud, which... I know that scares my family. But it's just how I feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of worsening of these depressive symptoms over the past fourteen months?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I can pinpoint specific times where it got worse. It feels like it's just been this... constant, heavy thing the whole time. I haven't really noticed it going up and down, just that it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, both, actually. Most of the time, I'm just... really slowed down. It takes me so long just to bathe and get dressed and make it to the door. My family says it looks like I'm moving underwater. But then, other times, I get this... I don't know, this restless feeling. I end up just pacing up and down the hallway, wringing my hands, and I just can't seem to settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where these depressive symptoms were so severe that you were unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's hard, and like I said, it takes me forever to get going, but I haven't had days where I physically couldn't get out of bed at all. I still manage to get up, even if it's just to sit on the couch or pace the hallway.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like you were at the lowest point, with your depressive symptoms being the worst they've ever been?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying before, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a specific low point where it suddenly got worse than usual. It's just... always severe, always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve or get better, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't ever really lifted. Looking back, most of this whole illness has just been spent under that heavy depression. Even during the times when the voices or the suspicious thoughts were quieter, that dark, heavy feeling was still there. It just hasn't gotten any better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your depressive symptoms were somewhat better for at least two months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, it's just been constant. I haven't had a break from it for two months, or even two weeks. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since your depressive symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've lost weight. So, yes, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness and depression present for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they are. It feels like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor. But no, I wouldn't say I feel worried or panicked.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities or hobbies that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to be pretty involved with things. I was always the one organizing rides for the choir, setting up garden swaps, or getting snacks together for the youth basketball games. But now... I just don't have the drive for any of it. I can sit there for hours just staring at my phone, and I don't even feel the push to answer anybody when they reach out. I just don't care about doing those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I... yeah. I just don't have the energy to fight the thought anymore that it might be better if I just didn't wake up. I've actually started saying it out loud, which... I know that scares my family. But it's just how I feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of worsening of these depressive symptoms over the past fourteen months?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I can pinpoint specific times where it got worse. It feels like it's just been this... constant, heavy thing the whole time. I haven't really noticed it going up and down, just that it's always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level, such as feeling restless or slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, both, actually. Most of the time, I'm just... really slowed down. It takes me so long just to bathe and get dressed and make it to the door. My family says it looks like I'm moving underwater. But then, other times, I get this... I don't know, this restless feeling. I end up just pacing up and down the hallway, wringing my hands, and I just can't seem to settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where these depressive symptoms were so severe that you were unable to get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's hard, and like I said, it takes me forever to get going, but I haven't had days where I physically couldn't get out of bed at all. I still manage to get up, even if it's just to sit on the couch or pace the hallway.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like you were at the lowest point, with your depressive symptoms being the worst they've ever been?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying before, it's just been this constant, heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't really had a specific low point where it suddenly got worse than usual. It's just... always severe, always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your depressive symptoms seemed to improve or get better, even if it was just for a little while?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn't ever really lifted. Looking back, most of this whole illness has just been spent under that heavy depression. Even during the times when the voices or the suspicious thoughts were quieter, that dark, heavy feeling was still there. It just hasn't gotten any better.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt like your depressive symptoms were somewhat better for at least two months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, it's just been constant. I haven't had a break from it for two months, or even two weeks. It's just always there.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since your depressive symptoms started?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've lost weight. Food has just... stopped meaning much to me lately. I guess I skip meals without even really noticing it. The only reason I realized is because my flagger pants for work are getting pretty loose on me, and my family started asking why I look so drawn out. So, yes, I've definitely lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness and depression present for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, they are. It's pretty much all day, every day. Most mornings, I wake up with this heavy... just this hollow sadness right in my chest. It feels like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor. And that feeling just stays with me the whole day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or having an inability to sit still when you're feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It actually kind of goes both ways. There are times when I just cannot settle down, and I end up pacing the hallway, just... wringing my hands. \n\nBut then other times, it's the complete opposite. Everything is just so slow. It takes me so long just to bathe, get dressed, and make it to the door. My family actually tells me it looks like I'm moving underwater.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience any pleasure when good things happen to you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I can just sit for hours staring at my phone when people reach out, and I just don't feel the push to answer anybody. None of it brings me any joy or pleasure anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: Not quite two years, no. It's been going on for about a year and eight months. It started right after my closest friend died.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
